2026年 10月 30日 全7頁

2026年 7-9月期 GDP（1次速報）予測 
～前期比年率＋1.5％を予想～ 
個人消費は小幅減だった一方、設備投資と輸出が実質GDPを押し上げ

経済調査部 エコノミスト 畑中 宏仁 
  チーフエコノミスト 神田 慶司

[要約] 
◼ 11月 16日公表予定の 2026年 7-9月期の GDP1次速報では、実質 GDPが前期比年率＋
1.5％（前期比＋0.4％）と、4四半期連続のプラス成長を予想する。個人消費は小幅
に減少したが、設備投資と輸出の増加が実質GDPを押し上げたようだ。 
◼ 個人消費はサービスが増加したものの、財が減少した影響の方が大きかったと予想す
る。設備投資は 4-6 月期に特殊要因で減少していた反動もあって増加したとみられる。
公共投資は減少、政府消費は増加したと見込んでいる。 
◼ 輸出と輸入はサービスが減少した一方、財が増加したと予想する。代替調達の進展に
よる鉱物性燃料輸入増加は実質 GDPを押し下げたが、石油国家備蓄の大規模放出が行
われなかったことで公的在庫変動が実質 GDP成長率を押し上げた分が相殺したようだ。

1．2026年 7-9月期の実質 GDP成長率は前期比年率＋1.5％を予想 
11月 16日公表予定の 2026年 7-9月期の GDP1次速報では、実質 GDPが前期比年率＋1.5％
（前期比＋0.4％）と、4四半期連続のプラス成長を予想する（10月30日正午までに公表された
基礎統計に基づく）。個人消費は小幅に減少したが、設備投資と輸出が実質GDPを押し上げたよ
うだ。 
個人消費はサービスが増加したものの、財が減少した影響の方が大きかったと予想する。設
備投資は4-6月期に特殊要因で減少していた反動もあって増加したとみられる。 
輸出と輸入はサービスが減少したものの、財が増加したと予想する。中東産原油の代替調達
の進展による鉱物性燃料輸入の増加は実質 GDPを押し下げたが、石油国家備蓄の大規模放出が
行われなかったことで公的在庫変動が実質GDP成長率を押し上げた分で相殺されたようだ。

図表 2：実質 GDP成長率と需要項目別寄与度の推移

民需：個人消費、住宅投資は減少の一方、設備投資は大幅な増加を予想 
個人消費は前期比▲0.1％と予想する。4-6月期のインバウンド（個人消費の控除項目）の実
績が上方改定されるであろうことを踏まえると、2 四半期連続のマイナスとなると見込んでい
る。サービスは増加したものの、耐久財と半耐久財、非耐久財がそろって減少したとみている。
耐久財では、自動車税の環境性能割廃止やエアコンの省エネ基準の引き上げの影響もあって 4-
6 月期に増加していた自動車や家電への支出が減少したようだ。半耐久財・非耐久財では衣服
やたばこ、ガソリンなどが増加したものの、食料品などが減少したとみられる。サービスは外
食や鉄道旅客、娯楽などが増加したようだ。 
住宅投資は前期比▲1.8％と予想する。国土交通省によると、7月の建設工事費デフレーター
（住宅総合、大和総研による季節調整値）が前月比＋6.8％と高い伸びを示す中にあっても、7、
8 月平均の新設住宅着工戸数（国土交通省による季節調整値）は、分譲住宅の増加に支えられ
-1.0

### Page 3

3 / 7

4-6月平均比＋1.0％となった（巻末の関連指標②上段右）。しかし、進捗ベースで計上される
GDP統計上は、建設資材等の価格上昇の影響により実質では減少したとみられる。 
設備投資は前期比＋2.0％と予想する。建設投資は減少したものの、機械投資と知的財産生
産物投資（ソフトウェア、研究開発など）が増加したようだ。研究開発投資の増加には、4-6
月期に特殊要因で研究開発サービスの受取（設備投資の控除項目）が大幅に増加したことの反
動も含まれる。経済産業省によると、資本財総供給指数は、国産品と輸入品が共に増加し、7-
9 月期は前期比＋4.9％となった（巻末の関連指標②中段左）。一方、建設投資は、国土交通省
によると、民間非居住用建築・土木の出来高（名目額、大和総研による季節調整値）の7、8月
平均は 4-6月平均比＋1.2％となったが、7月の建設工事費デフレーター（非住宅総合、大和総
研による季節調整値）は前月比＋7.3％と大きく上昇しており、実質では減少したとみられる。 
民間在庫変動は前期比寄与度▲0.0％ptと予想する。仮置き値の原材料在庫は同＋0.1％pt、
仕掛品在庫は同▲0.1％pt となった。その他では製品在庫が同＋ 0.1％pt、流通在庫が同▲
0.1％ptだったとみられる。

公需：公共投資は減少、政府消費は増加と予想 
公共投資は前期比▲1.0％と予想する。国土交通省によると、7、8 月平均の公共工事出来高
（名目額、大和総研による季節調整値）は 4-6月平均比▲2.2％と減少傾向にある（巻末の関連
指標②中段右）。25年度補正予算に盛り込まれた公共事業の執行が一服したとみられる。加え
て、工事費の上昇も重しとなっている。 
政府消費は前期比＋0.2％と予想する。退職金支払いの増加や私立高校授業料の実質無償化、
公立小学校の給食費無償化もあって高い伸びとなった 4-6月期に続き、医療費の増加により 7-
9月期の政府消費も増加したとみられる。 
公的在庫変動は前期比寄与度＋0.3％ptと予想する。4-6月期と異なり、7-9月期には大規模
な石油の国家備蓄放出は行われず、公的在庫変動は実質GDP成長率を押し上げたとみられる。

外需：輸入増が輸出増を上回り、外需寄与度は▲0.2％ptを予想 
輸出は前期比＋0.5％と予想する。サービスは減少したものの、財は増加したと見込んでい
る。財では半導体等製造装置や化学製品、鉱物性燃料などが増加したようだ。サービスでは、
4-6月期に大幅に増加していた研究開発サービスの反動減に加え、産業財産権等使用料やイン
バウンドなども減少したとみている。 
輸入は前期比＋1.8％と予想する。輸出と同様、サービスは減少したものの、財は増加した
と見込んでいる。財では、代替調達の進展により鉱物性燃料が大きく増加したほか、電算機類
も増加したようだ。サービスではアウトバウンドは増加したものの、研究開発サービスや著作
権等使用料などが減少したとみられる。

### Page 4

1

4 / 7

輸入増が輸出増を上回り、純輸出（外需）の実質GDP成長率に対する寄与度は前期比▲0.2％
ptになったと予想する。

2．10-12月期の実質 GDPはおおむね横ばいを予想 
2026年 10-12月期の実質 GDP成長率は前期比でおおむね横ばいになると見込んでいる。個人
消費は小幅に増加、設備投資は増加すると予想する。輸出はAI関連需要を背景とした米国向け
輸出を中心に増加するものの、輸入の伸びが上回り、外需はマイナスになるとみている。 
個人消費は小幅に増加すると予想する。厚生労働省「毎月勤労統計調査」の現金給与総額は
前年同月比で 3％を超える伸びが続いていることに加え、10月以降は各地で最低賃金が引き上
げられることから、名目賃金の高い伸びは続くだろう。一方、9月の消費者物価指数（除く生
鮮食品）は前年同月比＋2.1％と、8月から 0.4％pt伸び幅が拡大した。政策要因による影響を
差し引いたとしても、食料品等を中心に物価上昇率は高まっており、実質賃金上昇率を押し下
げるだろう。エネルギー価格についても、電気・ガス料金補助は12月使用分（1月請求分）か
ら再開されるものの、現行の補助は 9月使用分（10月請求分）で一旦終了するため、10-12月
期の物価は押し上げられる。加えて、ガソリン補助金の基準価格が12月以降引き上げられるこ
とや、燃料費・原料費調整制度によって資源価格高騰の影響がラグを持って電気・ガス料金に
反映されることも物価の押し上げ要因となろう1。 
設備投資は増加すると予想する。日本銀行「全国企業短期経済観測調査」 （日銀短観）によ
ると、9月調査時点での 2026年度の設備投資計画（全規模全産業、除く土地、含むソフトウェ
ア・研究開発）は前年度比＋9.3％と、前年の 9月調査時点（同＋9.5％）と同程度の伸びが示
された。建設資材価格と労務費の上昇が建設投資を下押しするものの、省力化投資やAI関連投
資といった設備投資の増加基調は続くと見込む。 
住宅投資は建設資材と労務費上昇の影響を受けて減少すると見込んでいる。公共投資は工事
費の高騰を背景に横ばい圏で推移すると予想する。政府消費は高齢化などに伴う医療費・介護
費増により増加すると見込んでいる。 
輸出は増加すると予想する。米国の設備投資は中東情勢をめぐる不透明感や金利上昇の中に
あっても、AI関連需要を背景に増加が続くだろう。米国内での設備投資の増加は米国向け輸出
の追い風となり、財輸出は増加すると見込んでいる。一方、インバウンドは中国政府による日
本への渡航自粛要請が続く中、小幅な増加にとどまるだろう。 
輸入は増加すると予想する。経済産業省によると、9月の原油調達について、バブ・エル・
マンデブ海峡経由で調達予定であった原油の輸送ルートをスエズ運河経由に切り替えたことで

1 12月以降の電気・ガス料金補助とガソリン補助金に関する政府の対応については、首相官邸「中東情勢を踏
まえた『緊急的激変緩和措置』の今後の対応についての会見」（2026年10月 29日）を参照。

### Page 5

1

5 / 7

到着が遅れた分は備蓄原油の既放出分で対応したという2。10月は前年平月比 10割の調達が実
現できる見通しとのことであり、11月以降も同量を確保できるのであれば、財輸入は押し上げ
られるだろう。 
リスク要因は中東情勢の悪化とAI関連需要の下振れである。中東情勢が悪化し、原油価格が
さらに高騰すれば、物価高を通じて個人消費を一段と下押しするだろう。また、足元の米国経
済では設備投資が景気を下支えしており、とりわけデータセンターや半導体などのIT関連投資
がけん引役となっている。10月半ばにかけて、一部AI関連企業の財務上の脆弱性が意識され、
株価が下落する局面があったが、こうした懸念がAI関連企業全体に広がれば、外部資金への依
存度が高い企業を中心に設備投資が鈍化し得る3。その場合、米国向け輸出が下押しされる可能
性があり、注意が必要だ。

2 詳細は、第 5回中東情勢に伴う重要物資の安定的な供給確保のためのタスクフォース（2026年 10月2日）
における経済産業省提出資料を参照。 
3 詳細は、矢作大祐・藤原翼「米国経済見通し CoreWeave問題をどうみるか」（大和総研レポート、2026年
10月22日）を参照。

Idea
Chroma Ate Inc.  | Asia Pacific
Momentum Seen Sustained into
2027
SL T, photonics (transceiver+CPO), and power testing should
continue to drive growth into 2027, with contributions from FT
handlers and burn-in ovens likely emerging as well. Stay OW.
3Q26 miss on GM: See details in Results Summary
Growth momentum seen sustained into 4Q26 and 2027: Management indicated
that the strong business momentum observed in 3Q26 is likely to stay intact going

forward, driven by both power testing and semi & photonics businesses. Normalized
GM remains at ~60% (2026 guidance of ~60% GM implies GM back to ~60% in
4Q26 vs. 57.5% in 3Q26).
• Power testing: 60-70% of revenue is AI-related for 2026, and it is expected
to be sustained into 2027. HVDC testing tools could be the major driver, and
it has already received orders from customers.
• SLT: 9M26 revenue doubled on a YoY basis. SL T demand used to focus on

one major customer, but management indicated that it has added AVGO and
MRVL as new customers. Moreover, demand in 4Q26/1H27 should be mostly
related to inference applications, including CPU/ASIC/LPU.
• Photonics: 9M26 revenue grew by 4x on  a YoY basis. Demand from
transceivers should remain solid and CPO should grow notably in 2027.
Chroma focuses on Insertion 3/4o and shipments for the mass production
line next year  are seen reaching 4-5x vs. that for the pilot line this year.

Moreover, it has added another customer for a pilot run in 9M27 and mass
production in 4Q27. At the industry level, if insertion is excluded from mass
production, Insertion 3 tool demand should be higher. Opportunities will
likely be much larger when CPO penetrates the scale-up network.
• New businesses: FT handlers have been qualified by multiple HPC/CSP
customers and orders have been placed. Burn-in ovens have secured the first
CSP customer, with more HPC customers expected to follow next year.

These should be new addressable markets for Chroma in the coming years.
Stay OW: We believe Chroma should continue to benefit from the AI compute/
networking/power testing opportunities due to its strong customer relationship,
solid execution and outstanding technology expertise. Our PT of NT$2,800 is based
on 40x 2027e P/E, implying a PEG of 0.6 (vs. 1.0 over the past decade).

Results Summary
3Q26 miss on GM:
Off revenue of  NT$17,926mn (+32% QoQ/+180% YoY), GM contracted 3ppt QoQ, to
57.5%, below our estimate at 60.5% and consensus at 62.7%, mainly owing to a less
favorable product mix.
Opex of NT$3,141mn (opex ratio 17.5%) was lower than our estimate at NT$3,797mn (opex
ratio 21.2%) and consensus at NT$3,321mn (opex ratio 21.7%), reflecting operating
leverage.
This led to operating profit of NT$7,167mn (OPM up 1.2ppt QoQ, to 40.0%), 2% higher

than our estimate at NT$7,040mn (OPM 39.3%) and 14% higher than consensus at NT
$6,281mn (OPM 41.0%).
With a narrower non-op gain of NT$186mn, net profit reached NT$5,647mn (EPS NT
$13.37), 2% short of our estimate at NT$5,790mn (EPS NT$13.71) and 6% short of
consensus at NT$5,317mn (EPS NT$12.59).

A Closer Look
Test instrument and ATS segment looks set to reach another
record high in 2026
Revenue for the segment increased 55% YoY to NT$10,546mn (37% of the mix) in 2025.
We estimate it will rise 118% YoY to NT$24,564mn (40% of the mix) in 2026. The ever-
increasing power consumption of AI clusters should continue to drive demand for higher
spec testing tools. Along with our forecast of a 75% CAGR for global power supply unit

demand in the coming three years (see our report), this should remain a major driver for
Chroma, in our view.

Multiple drivers in the semiconductor business
System-level testing  (SL T) business
SL T should benefit from the capex cycle of NVIDIA's Rubin generation GPU, AMD's MI450
GPU and more ASIC accelerators.  Management pointed out that discrete testing tools are
still preferred  by major customers.  We are also seeing more HPC chip designers adopting

the system level testing process to make sure defects won't be incorporated into the AI
systems. We believe Chroma, as the first mover in this market, should benefit from
increasing chip volume, as well as higher TDP.
Metrology business
The metrology business should continue to benefit from current advanced packaging
architectures from its foundry and OSAT customers. It is confident that the performance
of its tools can win future architecture projects, which our industry checks suggest should

have higher metrology density.

### Page 4

M
Idea
4
Exhibit 4: Chroma –  Offerings for Metrology in Advanced Packaging
Source: Chroma
Chroma's metrology equipment employs white light interferometry technology. The basic
working principle is that white light is emitted on the reference mirror and the sample,
which are placed at the same distance from the light source. The reflected beams from
the reference mirror and sample surface are then received by a light sensor.
Interference occurs when two light beams collide, which could cause  them to either

strengthen or weaken, depending on the phase of the two light beams. Thus, by
measuring the intensity of the combined light beams, the distance between light sensor
and multiple points on the target surface can be calculated through an algorithm. This can
then be used to measure the dimensions of the structure or the alignment of layers.
Exhibit 5: Concept of Light Interference
Source: Keyence
Exhibit 6: Setup of Interferometer
Source: Wikipedia
CPO contribution to start from 2026

Chroma has been qualified for optical engine testing in Insertion 3 and system-level
testing in Insertion 4, and there might be incremental opportunities if supply chain players'
progress falls behind for testing equipment in Insertion 2. Over the longer term, Chroma
also  aspires to participate in the PIC optical/electrical/reliability testing in Insertion 1.

FT handlers to add another revenue stream
In HPC testing, Chroma used to focus mostly on the SL T side, although it did unveil its first
HPC FT handler earlier this year and is now going through the qualification process. We
believe this is a natural extension of its expertise and customer relationships accumulated
in SL Ts. We expect Chroma to gain more traction in FT handlers for AI HPCs in 2027.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Chroma Ate Inc. (2360.TW)
Base case, 40x 2027e P/E. We use P/E as our valuation methodology because we think it better
captures Chroma's earnings growth momentum. The stock traded at an average valuation of
~19x P/E during 2014-25 with an operating profit CAGR of 20%, implying a PEG of 1.0. We think
our target multiple of 40x is justified by the 68% operating profit CAGR we project for

2025-28.
Risks to Upside
n Better-than-expected macro conditions
n Stronger semi and photonics and power testing demand
n Margin improvement via better product mix
n Better opex control
Risks to Downside
n Macro slowdown
n Softer-than-expected AI capex
n Slower progress in the advanced packaging capacity expansion
n Weaker consumer electronics and automobile related demand
n Margin contraction arising from lack of revenue scale and increasing opex

## Document 135: 261102_citi_auras

Source PDF: all report pdf/261102_citi_auras.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-02

### Page 1

Auras Technology (3324.TWO)
3Q26 Preview: Deferrals Weigh on 3Q; ASIC Ramp Underpins 
4Q26E/2027E Growth
CITI'S TAKE 
Auras is scheduled to report 3Q26E results in mid-Nov, followed by its 
annual investor meeting in the following weeks. 3Q26 revenue fell short of 
mgmt. ’s guidance of at least +15% QoQ on GB300 demand deferrals and 
Thailand flooding. We expect GPM below our prior forecast on lower scale 
and front-loaded expenses, and forecast 3Q26 net profit of NT$1.24bn, 13% 
below cons. We now model 4Q26E revenue growth of +20% QoQ on 
deferred demand and the ASIC liquid cooling ramp from Nov, and 2027E 
revenue growth of +50% YoY as multiple ASIC projects broaden the growth 
base. We raise our TP to NT$2,000 (20x 2027E EPS). Reiterate Buy.
3Q26 preview — Auras reported 3Q26 revenue of NT$9.8bn (+13% QoQ/+64% YoY), 
below mgmt. ’s earlier guidance. The shortfall mainly reflected customers deferring 
GB300 demand into 4Q26 and the flooding in Thailand. Given lower revenue scale 
and certain expenses recognized ahead of the upcoming ramp, we expect 3Q26E 
GPM to come in at 28.2% (-0.2 ppt QoQ/-1.6 ppts YoY), below our prior forecast of 
30.6%. With the opex ratio largely in line, we forecast 3Q26 net profit of NT$1.24bn 
(+28% QoQ/+27% YoY), and EPS of NT$13.32, 13% below cons.
Multiple ASIC projects to support 4Q26E/2027E — Mgmt. guided for 4Q26E 
revenue growth of +20% QoQ, driven by deferred 3Q26 revenue and the mass 
production of the ASIC project’s liquid cooling version, which we expect from Nov. 
We raise our 4Q26E growth forecast to +20% QoQ (from +16%), with GPM improving 
to 31.1%. While VR cold plate qualification is ongoing, we believe Auras is well 
positioned to win multiple ASIC projects, with a second project likely to contribute 
from 2H27. We now forecast 2027E revenue growth of +45% YoY, with GPM 
expanding to 32.2% on a rising share of customized projects and a better mix.
Implications — We lower our 2026E earnings forecast by 5% on the softer 3Q26. We 
also raise our 2027E/28E earnings forecasts by 13%/20% on higher ASIC liquid 
cooling shipment assumptions and margin expansion. Our revised TP of NT$2,000 
(from NT$1,800) remains based on 20x 2027E EPS. We believe recent concerns over 
the timing of various AI server projects are largely priced in. Given Auras’ structural 
growth in the server business, widening liquid cooling content across GPU and ASIC 
platforms, and an optimizing global manufacturing footprint, we view current 
valuation as attractive and reiterate our Buy rating.
n

Bull/Bear: Auras Technology (3324.TWO)
Prepared for Kevin Lu

### Page 6

Auras Technology (3324.TWO)
02 November 2026 Citi Research
6
Auras Technology
Company description
Auras Technology was founded in 1998 and focused on the design and
manufacturing of thermal modules for NB/PCs. It quickly became one of
Taiwan’s top two NB cooling component suppliers. Early products included
heatsinks, fins, heat pipes, and fans for NBs, PCs, and smartphones. As
device form factor evolved, the company invested in new technologies such

as ultra-thin vapor chamber in the 2000s to address the thermal challenges
of increasingly compact consumer electronics. During the 2010s, Auras
expanded into graphic card and server cooling and was an early adopter of
liquid cooling technology. By 2012, the company had begun developing liquid
cooling component and was among the first Taiwanese firms to venture into
AI server liquid cooling.
Investment strategy
We rate Auras a Buy. Auras is emerging as a key liquid-cooling supplier as

hyperscalers expand deployment. The company has secured qualifications for
cold plates and manifolds, opening access to a wider slate of Nvidia and CSP
projects. Thailand-based production offers non-China capacity at a scale
aligned with hyperscaler sourcing plans, allowing Auras to compete more
effectively for next-generation products.
Valuation
We value Auras using a PE-based approach, reflecting its accelerating
earnings trajectory and rising share in liquid cooling market of AI servers. Our

TP of NT$2,000 is based on 20x 2027E EPS, which we believe is justified
given the company’s expanding server revenue base, deeper engagement
with CSP customers, and broader product portfolio across cold plates, CDMs,
and CDUs.
Risks
This stock is High Risk based upon our quantitative model, but assigning a
High Risk rating is not supported in our view by other qualitative factors such
as rapid RVL progress and Thailand expansion, and so a High Risk rating has

not been applied. Key downside risks to our investment thesis and target
price include: 1) slower adoption of liquid cooling; 2) heightened price
competition; 3) lower-than-expected share in liquid cooling components;
and 4) emerging alternative cooling technologies.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.

群聯(8299)法說會前財報預估 
受惠 AI 帶動儲存需求，4Q26 營運動能延續

| 財報訊息   
群聯即將於 11/6(五)舉行法說會， 3Q26營收季增 26.79%至860.7億
元， 優於預期 ，市場普遍預估毛利率介於56~65%，EPS約124.22元，
關注公司對於2027年NAND市況、產業供需看法以及AI生態系佈局
與應用。 
| 營運現況  
3Q26營收季增26.79%，優於預期：3Q26營收季增26.79%至860.7億
元，優於預期，主要反映AI基礎建設、AI推論及企業級儲存需求快
速成長，2Q26公司AI Ecosystem modules 占比38%，然而9月公司AI 
Ecosystem modules 營收貢獻度已超過整體營收50%，考量在NAND
成本推升下，推估毛利率64.5%，稅後EPS 134.52元。 
| 營運展望  
預估4Q26營收季增 11.92%：預估 4Q26營收季增 11.92%至963.3億
元，動能來自持續受惠AI帶動儲存、運算、網路及相關基礎建設需
求，9月公司AI Ecosystem modules包括 eSSD 、aiDAPTIV solution、
AI PC、AI Networking、AI Server及Boot Drive開機碟等AI相關應用，
營收貢獻度已超過整體營收50%，反映公司Phison 3.0策略轉型，優
化產品結構 ， 目前公司近9成在於高附加價值市場應用 ， 跳脫以往傳
統零售業務景氣波動影響。先前法說公司提及目前 NAND供應短缺
仍是最大限制，預期2H26價格仍維持漲勢，考量隨著購入NAND成
本墊高下，推估毛利率62.4%，稅後EPS 142.08元。展望2027年，隨
著AI推理從文本、圖像、影片走向AI Agent產生TB級資料量，將推
升高容量、高效能儲存需求，將支撐公司後續營運動能。

## Document 236: [012450] 한화에어로스페이스(012450) 3Q26_ 그래도 믿는다한화에어로스페이스(012450) 3Q26_ 그래도 믿는다3Q26_ 그래도 믿는다 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[012450] 한화에어로스페이스(012450) 3Q26_ 그래도 믿는다한화에어로스페이스(012450) 3Q26_ 그래도 믿는다3Q26_ 그래도 믿는다 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

2026.10.30   
한화에어로스페이스  
(012450)

3Q26: 그래도 믿는다  Company Brief

3분기 매출 6조 943억원/영업이익 4,520억원 
한화에어로스페이스의 3분기 매출은 6조 943억원/영업이익 4,520억원(OPM 7.4%)로 
컨센서스와 당사 추정치를 모두 대폭 하회하는 실적을 기록하였다. 앞서 실적을 발표한 
한화오션의 실적 쇼크를 감안하더라도, 주력사업인 지상방산 부문의 실적이 매출 1조 
2,455억원/영업이익 217억원(1.7%)로 지난 분기(2조 1,075억원/ 5,330억원/ 25.3%) 
는 물론 전년동기(2조 1,098억원/5,725억원/27.1%)보다도 현저히 감소하였다. 
일회성 요인은 대전사업장 폭발사고에 따른 복구비용 등 300억원, 일부 국내사업 
충당금 160억원 및 환율하락에 따른 효과 등이 있었으나 역시 이를 감안해도 내수와 
수출 모두 실질 이익률은 5%미만으로 추정된다. 지상방산 분기 매출은 1분기(1조 
2,211억)보다 오히려 소폭 증가하였으나 영업이익이 1분기 2,087억원에서 
217억원으로 대폭 감소한 점에 대하여 회사는 프로젝트별 믹스 구성에 따른 낮은 
이익률의 인도분이 반영되었다고 설명하고 있으나, 그럼에도 불구하고 수출과 내수 
이익률 모두 BEP에 가까웠다는 점은 쉽게 이해가 가지는 않는다. 다만 방산기업의 
특성상 대전사업장 사고에 따른 실제 납기 영향 등 회사가 공개할 수 없는 사정이 
있었을 것으로 추측할 뿐이다. 대전사업장은 현재 복구작업이 진행 중이며 27년 
상반기 중에는 가동 정상화할 것으로 예상된다.  
긍정적인 소식도 있다. 폴란드향 천무의 연간 인도대수가 기존 가이던스인 40대에서 
60대로 증가하였으며, 이를 포함하여 3분기에 인도되지 않은 물량의 대부분이 
4분기에 집중될 예정으로, 올해의 4분기 실적 개선폭은 통상적으로 볼 수 있는 
4분기의 계절적 집중도보다 더 커질 것으로 예상한다. 방산기업의 실적은 인도일정에 
따라 크게 변할 수 있는 분기 실적보다 연간 합산이 더 중요하다는 점을 한 번 더 
믿고, 4분기를 기다리는 게 좋겠다.

투자의견 BUY 유지, 목표주가 1,200,000원 하향  
투자의견 BUY를 유지하지만, SOTP기반 목표주가는 기존 1,490,000원에서 
1,200,000원으로 19.5% 하향한다. 비영업가치 중 자회사 한화시스템과 한화오션, 
쎄트렉아이의 지분가치 하락(합산 8.7조원→7.1조원), 그리고 지난 분기 대비 늘어난 
별도 순차입금(3.25조→4.95조원)을 반영하였으며 최근 방산섹터 전반의 밸류 하락을 
감안하여 영업가치 중 지상방산+항공우주의 Target P/E를 기존 35배에서 30배로 
조정하였다. 
3분기는 여러모로 악재가 겹쳤다. 주식시장 전반의 하락에 더해 방산섹터 수주 지연에 
따른 디레이팅, 그리고 납기 지연에 따른 실적 하락 등 나올 수 있는 악재는 다 
나왔다고도 볼 수 있다. 그러나 뒤집어보면 지연중인 수주는 시간의 문제일 뿐 가시화 
가능성이 매우 높으며, 실적 또한 진행률 인식이 아닌 인도기준 인식에 따른 일시적 
현상으로 4분기 이후 복구될 가능성이 크다. 펀더멘털의 유의미한 변화는 없다고 볼 
수 있으며 밸류에이션 매력도가 증가한 현 시점에서 추가 매수를 고려할 때다. 다만, 
대전사업장 사고에 따른 당국의 제재조치가 아직 확정되지 않았음은 잔여 리스크 
요인이다. 동종사의 신규계약 입찰제한 제재조치가 주가에 상당한 영향을 미쳤음을 
고려하면 한화에어로스페이스 또한 제재조치 여부를 지켜본 후 투자할 것을 권한다.

당 보고서 공표일 기준으로 해당 기업과 관련하여, 
 회사는 해당 종목을 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 금융투자분석사와 그 배우자는 해당 기업의 주식을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 당 보고서는 기관투자가 및 제 3자에게 E-mail등을 통하여 사전에 배포된 사실이 없습니다.  
 회사는 6개월간 해당 기업의 유가증권 발행과 관련 주관사로 참여하지 않았습니다. 
 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
 당 연구원은 한화에어로스페이스(012450)의 일부 비용을 지원받아 한화에어로스페이스 공장 투어(2026년 10월 1-2)를 다녀왔습니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

[투자의견] 
종목추천 투자등급  
종목투자의견은 향후 12개월간 추천일 종가대비 해당종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함.  
 Buy(매수): 추천일 종가대비 +15% 이상  
 Hold(보유): 추천일 종가대비 -15% ~ 15% 내외 등락 
 Sell(매도): 추천일 종가대비 -15% 이상

 Overweight(비중확대)

두산에너빌리티 (034020)

EARNINGS REVIEW Company Analysis | 건설/원전 | 2026. 10. 30  
숫자로 증명하는 중

3Q26 Review: 에너빌부문 믹스개선, 두산밥캣 호실적 
두산에너빌리티 3분기 매출액은 4조 4,494억원으로 전년동기대비 14.7% 증가, 컨센
서스에 부합했다. 영업이익은 2,612억원으로 전년동기대비 90.6% 증가, 컨센서스 영
업이익 2,464억원 대비 6.0% 상회하는 호실적을 기록했다. 에너빌리티부문의 원자
력, 가스터빈 매출 증가에 따른 믹스 개선 효과와 두산밥캣의 가격정책 효과에 힘입은 
마진 개선으로 안정적인 실적 기조가 이어지고 있어 긍정적이다. 분기 환평가손실로 
인해 순이익은 여타 수출기업과 유사하게 컨센서스 대비 하회했다. 에너빌부문의 수주
는 누계 10조원으로 목표대비 75%를 채웠으며, 수주 대기중인 안건들을 고려할 때 
당분간 수주 업사이클이 몇년간 지속될 것임이 분명한 시점이다. 
기회가 아닌 경쟁력, 모멘텀이 아닌 수주  
두산에너빌리티에 대해 투자의견 Buy 유지하나, 목표주가를 95,000원으로 하향했다. 
목표 EBITDA는 2035년 EBITDA에 WACC 8%를 적용, 목표 EV/EBITDA는 Global 
Peers인 GE Vernova의 2026년 EV/EBITDA 멀티플에서 자회사 실적 변동성을 고
려해 10% 할인 적용했다. 호실적과 수주 증가에도 GEV의 멀티플 감소와 순차입금 
증가로 인한 NAV 감소를 밸류에이션에 반영한 결과이다. 전일 주가는 한수원의 체코 
원전 시공사 협상 결렬로 인해 7.65% 하락 마감했다. 시공 수주는 아쉽지만 두산에너
빌리티의 체코 원전 기자재 납품에는 전혀 영향이 없으며, 글로벌 전력 수요 증가로 경
제적해자인 대형 가스 터빈 수급 쇼티지는 당분간 지속될 전망임을 고려할 때 전일 주
가는 낙폭 과대로 판단한다. 1) 두산에너빌리티의 가스터빈은 이미 2031년 출하 물량
에 대한 예약 주문이 마무리되고 있으며, 2) 또한 AP1000의 우량한 수주 파이프라인
과, 3) 뉴스케일파워, 테라파워, 롤스로이스, X Energy 등의 SMR 제작을 위한 협업 역
시 지속되고 있다는 점에 주목한다. 전일 기사를 통해 뉴스케일파워 SMR 1호 제작 계
약을 체결했다는 소식을 전했으며, 이제 에너지 트렌지션은 기회가 아닌 회사의 경쟁
력으로, 가스터빈과 원전은 모멘텀이 아니라 수주로 입증되고 있다.  
 Analyst 김세련 
sally.kim@ls-sec.co.kr

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Consumers Feel One Way, but Spend Another
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
Americans have spent much of the past several years telling surveys they are cautious
about the economy. Yet they continue to spend as though they are not. The University
of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index remains near multidecade lows, while the
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence has also been in a yearslong downtrend.

These surveys reflect a list of persistent concerns from elevated food and gas prices to
geopolitical uncertainty and labor market anxiety.
Even so, consumer spending has remained remarkably resilient. The final estimate of Q2
real consumer spending, as measured by PCE, was revised up to an annualized rate of
3.8%, well above the 2.3% 20-year average. More recent data suggest that momentum
has continued. According to Bank of America Institute, the latest monthly total credit and

debit card spending data per household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY) in September, and
spending excluding gasoline was up 5.1% YoY, both the third strongest growth rates in the
past four years. 1
The persistence of consumer spending is particularly notable given that temporary cyclical
boosts like larger tax refunds are starting to fade. That raises an important question: What is
actually funding strong consumption today? Recent data suggest the drivers are becoming

increasingly more diversified, with broadening labor market trends, healthy consumer
finances and elevated wealth effects all reinforcing one another and underpinning consumers.
First, the labor market continues to provide an important foundation for consumer spending,
and recent trends suggest wage growth is supporting a broader set of households than in
recent years. Jobs growth has slowed from the exceptionally strong pace seen following the
pandemic, but broader indicators suggest labor market conditions remain relatively stable.

Although the September jobs report came in softer than expected, the three- and six-month
rolling averages indicate that the trend in jobs growth likely bottomed out last year and has
gradually improved since (Exhibit 1A). Meanwhile, the unemployment rate remains low by
historical standards and layoffs remain contained. And while wage growth has moderated
and recently trailed headline inflation (Exhibit 1B), cumulative income gains over the past
several years have likely helped many households absorb higher prices better.

An important development though has been improving wage growth trends among lower-
income households, where spending tends to be most sensitive to changes in wages. Bank
of America Institute consumer deposit account data show after-tax wage growth for lower-
income households outpaced higher-income households for a second consecutive month
in August, rising 4.7% YoY compared with 3.5% for higher-income households. Importantly,
this improvement appears to be supporting discretionary spending, not just spending

on necessities. Lower-income discretionary spending rose 5.7% YoY in August, nearly
matching the 5.9% pace for higher-income households. In other words, the narrowing gap
in wage and spending growth across income cohorts suggests consumer resilience looks
to be becoming less dependent on affluent households alone, creating a broader and more
durable foundation for consumption.
Second, household finances remain in relatively good shape, providing consumers with the

flexibility to continue spending. The ratio of household debt to disposable personal income
remains near its lowest level since the late 1990s, excluding the temporary distortions
caused by pandemic-related income support in 2020 and 2021 (Exhibit 1C). Recent
revisions to the Bureau of Economic Analysis national income accounts also suggest
consumers have a larger financial buffer than previous data suggested. Personal saving
estimates over the past year have been revised higher by $320 billion, or 39%, and the

revised savings rate remains above 4% after previous estimates showed it fell to 3% during
the summer.2 Concerns that consumers may be rapidly exhausting their savings buffers
may therefore be overstated. Supporting this view, Bank of America Institute consumer
1 Bank of America Institute, “Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers.” As of October 9, 2026.
2 Bank of America, “Massive Savings Markup = Another Reason to Be Bullish.” As of October 2, 2026.

Investment Implications
Consumer spending strength
remains a key pillar of economic
and earnings growth, which keeps
us constructive on our Equity
overweight. The growing connection
between household wealth and
spending reinforces the importance
of maintaining a diversified portfolio.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
deposit account data show that middle- and lower-income households continue to maintain
deposit balances above prepandemic levels across age groups.
Third, elevated household wealth continues to provide a powerful tailwind for consumer
spending, particularly among households that account for an outsized share of overall
consumption. U.S. household net worth rose $12.8 trillion (T) to a record $195.9T in Q2,

driven largely by gains in Equities, which contributed $10.7T of the increase (Exhibit 1D).
As a result, household net worth relative to disposable income climbed to a record 8.3,
meaning households now hold more than eight dollars of wealth for every dollar of annual
disposable income. Households are also sitting on $35.8T in home equity, an all-time high
level that provides a significant balance sheet cushion. Rising asset values have helped to

support spending through what economists refer to as wealth effects. In other words, when
households feel wealthier, they tend to be more willing to spend.
For investors, the key takeaway is that consumer spending is being supported by multiple
engines despite the weaker sentiment data. Labor income trends are supporting wider
participation, healthy balance sheets are helping households absorb economic pressures,
and elevated household wealth continues to sustain higher-income spending. Together,

these forces are supportive of economic growth and corporate profits, which is particularly
important since the consumer accounts for roughly 70% of U.S. gross domestic product.
That said, it does not eliminate risk. A meaningful risk to the consumer would be a scenario
in which both the labor and wealth engines weaken simultaneously. For now, labor market
conditions remain stable, and wealth effects remain supportive. Equity market pullbacks in
recent years have done little to derail consumer spending, suggesting it would likely take a

deeper and more prolonged drawdown to alter consumer behavior.
Exhibit 1: Consumer Spending is Being Supported by Multiple Engines.
A) Jobs growth has stablized. B) Wage growth have helped offset higher prices.

### Page 4

4 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
The Bull Market Turns Four: Older, Unloved—But Still Kicking
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Four years ago this month, virtually no one was in the mood to buy U.S. Equities, and for
good reasons.
Rewind to October 2022: Inflation the month before clocked in at an annual rate of 8.2%.

The yield curve was inverted, a signal that has preceded most past recessions. Consumer
sentiment was abysmal, having dropped to a record low in June 2022. Recession
concerns were mounting, with an October Wall Street Journal poll survey putting the
odds of a recession within 12 months at about 63%. And realizing (belatedly) that
inflation was not “transitory,” the Fed was in the midst of administering one of the most
aggressive monetary tightening cycles in decades, raising the fed funds rate five times

between March and September 2022 by a stunning 300 basis points.
Reflecting the mood at the moment, the S&P 500 closed at 3,577 on October 12, 2022,
down 25% below its January peak, leaving investors battered and bruised but blind to the
fact that the market had bottomed. Four years on, the bull market that emerged from
the gloom of 2022 is still standing and kicking, with the S&P 500 up roughly 130% since
the October 2022 lows.
The more than doubling of the S&P over the past four years has hardly been linear—and

has been accompanied with plenty of pain and doubt. Exhibit 2 maps the rocky path
trodden by the bull over the past four years. Less than six months into the rally, the
markets were rocked by the failure of Silicon Valley Bank (SVB) on March 10, 2023. The
S&P Regional Banks Select Industry Index lost 18% that week and stoked concerns of
massive deposit runs and credit stress across the capital markets. However, owing to
emergency intervention by the government, lower yields and strength elsewhere in the

markets, the crisis passed. Some banks failed that month, but the bull market didn’t.
Exhibit 2: Revisiting the Rocky Path Trodden by the Bull.
Period "Reason to Sell" What happened instead
October 2022 Inflation >8%, aggressive Fed tightening, recession concerns Bull market begins
March 2023 SVB/regional banking crisis Stocks keep climbing
2023-24 Inverted yield curve, “recession inevitable” Economy keeps expanding
2024 “Magnificent 7* bubble,” narrow breadth, expensive valuations Earnings propel market higher

2025 Tariffs, trade war, policy uncertainty Market absorbs another shock
2026 Oil/diesel shock, 5%+ yields, Fed tightening, geopolitical risks Bull reaches fourth birthday
*Apple, Amazon, Alphabet, Nvidia, Meta, Microsoft and Tesla. Source: Chief Investment Office. As of October 9, 2026.
But recessionary concerns hung like threatening clouds over the markets for most of
2023. Aggressive Fed tightening, an inverted yield curve, and soft economic data were

all taken as harbingers of an economic slowdown and profits recession. Yet the S&P 500
continued to climb in 2023, up 26.3% for the year, driven in large part by the outsized
price performance of the Magnificent Seven.
Investors spent much of 2024 fretting about a market too dependent on technology,
and therefore a market too narrow and too expensive. The unwinding of the Japan carry
trade in August 2024 engendered more angst, as did uncertainties around the 2024

U.S. presidential election. But despite a host of misgivings, the S&P 500 delivered total
returns of 25% in 2024. The unloved bull marched on.
Total returns in 2025 were robust as well—roughly 18%—notwithstanding—yet
again—a number of headwinds. In late January, DeepSeek, a cheaper China AI model,
raised questions about America’s technology leadership and the need to spend enormous
sums on chips and data centers. The Nasdaq promptly dropped by 3.1% thereafter.

Investment Implications
The adoption phase of the AI buildout
should translate to broader earnings
growth and performance across
sectors, reinforcing our conviction
in U.S. Equities. Given a strong
fundamental backdrop, we would
continue to buy on weakness as the
bull enters its fifth year

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
January’s jolt was followed by “Liberation Day,” or the warning of punishing U.S. tariffs
on America’s major trading partners. The trade-policy shock of April 2025 caused the
S&P 500 to drop nearly 19% from its February high to its April 8 low on uncertainty
that higher import costs would decrease corporate margins and consumer spending and
upend global supply chains. Amid the trade tumult, Treasury prices fell and the U.S. dollar

weakened on concerns that foreigners were abandoning U.S. assets. Remember all the
angst over the dollar debasement trade? If that wasn’t enough, expensive valuations and
enormous capital commitments raised doubts about the AI trade and triggered talk of an
AI bubble. Yet despite this wall of worry, the S&P 500, for the third year running, posted
double-digit gains in 2025.
And double-digit gains could be in store this year, with the S&P 500 up 14% through

October 9. The bull has rumbled on, taking in stride the energy shock from the war in
the Middle East, the shift in interest rate expectations from the Fed (from rate cuts
to rate hikes), and the rising uncertainty associated with the midterm elections. These
headwinds have been countered by tailwinds such as better-than-expected corporate
earnings and better-than-anticipated real growth.
Finally, as Exhibit 3 makes clear, what has given this bull so much muscle has been

the U.S. Technology sector, specifically the Magnificent 7. Powering the bull has been
the Magnificent 7, with a staggering 344% return since the 2022 lows, while U.S.
technology has delivered a 277% return versus the S&P 500’s return of 130%. Note the
outperformance of the S&P 500 relative to the main international indices.
Looking forward: Is the bull’s greatest strength also its Achilles’ heel? Bull
markets don’t die of natural causes. And they typically don’t expire because of one

particular signal or indicator. Bull markets turn to bear markets when a cluster of signals/
metrics turn south. Think deteriorating and downward earnings revisions, higher interest
rates, widening credit spreads and tighter lending standards, growth disappointments,
stretched valuations and sentiment, deteriorating market breadth, and an external
shock. Any one of these signals could flash red and not trouble the bull.  But when they
converge or feed off each other, that’s trouble.

In addition, since a great deal of the earnings growth, market leadership and capital
expenditures (capex) spending of this bull market have come from the technology sector,
the bull’s greatest strength could become its greatest vulnerability. AI has become an
investment ecosystem unto itself, and hence the fifth year of the bull market will pivot
in part around AI revenue, frontier model adoption rates, infrastructure spending, free
cash flows, depreciation costs, debt issuance, data center utilization, cloud growth, and

competition via cheaper models and computing alternatives. To put it mildly, there is a
great deal at stake when it comes to the bull and AI.
However, this is where we stand today: We believe the bull will continue to rumble
ahead next year, with AI revenues and productivity gains justifying the costs of the AI
infrastructure. Critically, AI is moving from the buildout phase, dominated by chipmakers
and hyperscaler capex, into the adoption phase, where software firms, healthcare,

finance, energy and a myriad of other firms start to see productivity gains and margin
expansion from rising AI adoption rates. We believe this will give better breadth to the
S&P 500; so too will continued profit growth across various U.S. sectors, AI investment
that starts to pay off, and an economy capable of absorbing setbacks or periodic bouts
of uncertainty.
The path forward won’t be linear—and there will be fits and starts, and market
drawdowns. That’s normal for any market. But that aside, the bull will keep rumbling,

roaming and kicking in 2027.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What is our view of the macro and market landscape heading
into 2027 and beyond?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Office
A supply side-led economic expansion continues, driven by capital investment in the AI
buildout, solid consumer spending and fiscal tailwinds still blowing, albeit with less force.
This creates a backdrop that keeps nominal growth well-above trend and, importantly,

above the interest cost on national debt.
The interest rate backdrop largely remains the same as of today, but, instead of the
sharp increases that we experienced in 2026, the curve should settle down, and yields
should remain relatively range bound. The Fed is likely to tap on the breaks a couple of
more times before reassessing new inflation, growth and employment trends later in
2027.
Concerns surrounding credit risk and "circular" financing are likely to rise the further we

move into the AI buildout, but we do not expect a default cycle to develop. Rather, we
expect the sharp backup in yields to place pressure namely on lower-quality credits in
areas having a difficult time building the cash flow necessary to pay the higher interest
costs.
We expect a broadening out within the market but technology to still maintain its
heavyweight positioning. As we move from training and evolving the large language
models to agentic, and physical AI new leaders are likely to develop. In addition, we

anticipate AI adopters such as biotechnology, transport, logistics, advance manufacturing
and financial companies to enter new growth and productivity phases.
Lastly, overall earnings growth in the U.S. should be low to mid double-digit percent
stages in 2027, which is about half of the growth from 2026 thus far but still around
double from normal levels.
Credit risks developing and higher energy prices for longer are the main risks to our bull
case for Equities extending further into 2027 and beyond.

Index Definitions
ecurities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

University of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index is a monthly survey of consumer confidence levels in the United States conducted by the University of Michigan.
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence measures what consumers are feeling about their expected financial situation, whether that's optimistic or pessimistic.
Consumer Price Index is a statistical measure that tracks the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a representative "market basket" of everyday goods and

services.
Nasdaq Composite Index is a stock market measure that tracks the performance of more than 3,000 stocks listed on the Nasdaq exchange.
MSCI Emerging Markets c is a stock market index that tracks the performance of large-cap and mid-cap companies across 24 developing and fast-growing nations.
MSCI All-Country World Index ex USA is a market-capitalization-weighted stock market index that tracks large- and mid-cap equity performance across developed and emerging markets

worldwide, excluding the United States.
MSCI Europe, Australasia, and the Far East (EAFE) Index is a stock market index that measures the performance of large- and mid-cap companies across 21 developed.
Dow Jones Industrial Average Index is a stock market index that tracks 30 major public companies listed on the New York Stock Exchange (NYSE) and Nasdaq.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index is an alternative version of the standard S&P 500 that gives every constituent company an identical weight of 0.2% during each quarterly rebalance, rather

than weighting companies by their market capitalization.
Russell 2000 Index is a stock market index that tracks the performance of approximately 2,000 small-cap (small-capitalization) public companies in the United States.
S&P 500 sub-sectors and industry groups Global Industry Classification Standard (GICS®)/S&P 500 Total Return Index, including Information Technology Total Return (TR) USD;
Consumer Discretionary TR USD; Industrials TR USD; Real Estate TR USD; Communication Services TR USD; Materials  TR USD; Financials TR USD; Consumer Staples TR USD; Utilities TR USD;

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

## Document 353: How the blue wave could reshape US energy and data centres

Energy aﬀordability shaped the midterms. A divided
government will now face pressure to shield consumers from
rising data centre power costs while speeding up new energy
supply. Onshoring and trade protections will continue, despite
higher near-term costs, as energy security remains a priority
Coco Zhang
After the midterm elections, the government will need to minimise data
centre-related costs for consumers, bring new power supply online faster and

protect supply chains. Pictured: Data centre in Ohio
Exit polls from the US midterm elections highlighted a key concern
shaping voters’ decisions: energy aﬀordability. Nearly 90% of
voters said the cost of living was a crucial or major factor in their
vote for Congress, with many pointing to gasoline and utility costs.
Meanwhile, as the AI data centre boom strains power supply and
infrastructure, 56% said the issue was a crucial or major factor in

their vote.
With Democrats now set to control both the House and Senate,
what does this mean for US energy and data centre policy?

### Page 2

Newly elected lawmakers will face pressure to address these
concerns, requiring the Democratic Congress and Republican White
House to ﬁnd common ground on shielding consumers from data
centre-related costs and accelerating new power supply, including
through permitting reform. Onshoring and supply chain protections
are also likely to continue. While they can raise near-term costs,
they remain important to US energy security. Developments in all
three areas could have signiﬁcant implications for businesses.

That said, Congress needs to strike a balance when drafting
legislation aimed at achieving these goals. More aggressive
measures could face a veto from President Trump.
Strengthened data centre regulation ahead,
but the devil is in the detail
The data centre industry is facing heightened local opposition.
Recent polls suggest that around one in seven Americans is
concerned about or opposes data centre development in their
neighbourhoods.
In response to this issue, there is likely to be tighter data centre

regulation. State-level policy will be more important to watch, as
action is politically and practically easier at this level. The most
likely approach is targeted regulation, much of which is already
emerging. This includes data centre-related taxes, reduced tax
incentives, environmental standards, and requirements for
developers to bear relevant cost increases. Outright moratoriums
remain unlikely to become mainstream. Where they are introduced,

they are more likely to be temporary, giving states time to develop
a data centre policy framework.
At the federal level, the key unanswered question is how much
authority should remain with the states and whether federal
legislation should mandate state regulations or simply provide
guidance. Republicans generally favour letting states lead, while
Democrats tend to support a stronger federal role.
We think sweeping data centre legislation is unlikely because of

President Trump’s veto power. Federal action is more likely to focus

on aﬀordability by protecting electricity customers from higher

to broader legislation, such as must-pass spending bills needed to
avoid a government shutdown, or a bipartisan package like

More broadly on AI, we expect the Democratic Party to increase

and beyond.
However, tighter AI and data centre regulation is unlikely to
fundamentally alter the industry’s growth trajectory. AI’s
importance to US economic growth and competition with China will
remain powerful drivers of expansion.
Business implications
State-level policy would more directly aﬀect project siting,
which is important for data centre companies to watch.
Where state rules are tighter, project development could slow.

However, a more restrictive regulatory environment could
also increase the value of data centres already approved or
under construction, easing some of the industry’s capital
constraints. Higher valuations could also encourage
developers to be more selective and expand at a more
measured pace, ultimately improving the quality of the
project pipeline.
Developers should also treat community engagement as a
strategic investment. Recent surveys show that while only

20-30% of Americans support local data centre construction,
support can rise to 50-70% when there are accompanying
community investments, such as in public infrastructure
improvements or reductions in residents' costs. More
developers are now signing binding community beneﬁt
agreements as part of project development.

Enabling faster power generation through
permitting reform
Both parties support timely expansion of electricity supply to meet

rising demand from AI and manufacturing reshoring. They also
generally agree that energy abundance can help reduce cost
pressures. While Democrats and Republicans diﬀer on the preferred
energy mix, there is still common ground. Technologies likely to
retain bipartisan support include nuclear, geothermal, carbon
capture, and biofuels.
However, the greatest potential for policy action may lie not in
speciﬁc technologies, but in permitting reform.
Today, many energy projects can spend over ﬁve years navigating

the federal permitting process. Around $240bn to $280bn of
infrastructure investment across the eight sectors below enters the

With both parties recognising the need for reform, the Senate
introduced the Bipartisan American Aﬀordability and Jobs Act
(BAAJA) in September 2026. Key permitting proposals include:

Speeding up approvals for energy projects
Providing greater certainty and consistency across
administrations
Making it easier to permit power transmission lines
Despite debate over its environmental impact, there is broad
agreement that the BAAJA would meaningfully improve the
permitting process. The key question is whether it can become law
under the current Congress. The Senate is expected to vote on 9
November, after which the bill would still need approval from the

House and President Trump. Democrats may want to delay the bill
and seek changes once they regain control of Congress next year,
but doing so could increase the risk of a presidential veto. This
creates an incentive to reach a deal sooner rather than later.

### Page 6

Even if the BAAJA does not pass under the current Congress,
permitting reform is likely to remain a bipartisan theme. The
growing need to expand power supply and infrastructure more
quickly will keep pressure on future Congresses to streamline
permitting.
Business implications
For energy companies – both renewables and fossil fuels –
the BAAJA could signiﬁcantly shorten permitting timelines. If
enacted, it would give project developers greater certainty,

reducing the risk of litigation and permit cancellations. This
could further expand project siting options for developers.
Nevertheless, in the very near term, projects may need to
retain ﬂexibility around their permitting strategy while the
new policy framework is developed and implemented.
Expect more protectionism and reshoring
efforts against China dominance
From clean energy technologies to the critical minerals that
underpin much of the US economy, China controls a large share of

global production capacity. Consequently, reducing reliance on
China will remain a US policy priority. Such eﬀorts would increase
rather than reduce project costs in the near term. But the long-term
beneﬁts of stronger domestic manufacturing and more secure
supply chains could outweigh those costs.
Global supply chain capacity distribution
across various sectors

### Page 7

Source: ING Research, BloombergNEF, International Energy Agency
This creates two key risks. First, heavy reliance on China leaves US
industries vulnerable to geopolitical tensions. We have already seen
this with rare earths, where Chinese export restrictions contributed
to disruptions for US auto manufacturers. Second, China’s supply
chain dominance can put US manufacturers at a competitive
disadvantage.
Building more secure energy and natural resource supply chains

has long attracted bipartisan support in the US. The Trump
administration has supported domestic rare earth production
through funding, purchase agreements, and equity investments. In
solar, China-focused trade and tax credit restrictions are also
supporting the expansion of domestic manufacturing.
We expect this focus to continue. The executive branch could
pursue further protective measures and is unlikely to face major
opposition from Congress. Congress could also authorise additional

funding to strengthen rare earth supply chains.
Furthermore, a Democratic-controlled Congress could push to
extend clean manufacturing tax credits as part of broader
negotiations with President Trump. Given the administration’s focus
on countering China and expanding US manufacturing, an
extension could emerge as a compromise in a broader legislative
package under an optimistic scenario. All these eﬀorts would also
extend into future administrations.

This publication has been prepared by ING solely for information purposes

objectives. The information does not constitute investment recommendation,
and nor is it investment, legal or tax advice or an oﬀer or solicitation to
Business implications
For US companies:
Clean energy companies and other industries that rely on
rare earths, including semiconductors, AI, defence, and

electronics, should have enough policy certainty to build
long-term supplier relationships outside China. In the near
term, however, diversiﬁcation could raise supply chain costs
as alternative sources may be more expensive. Solar could
face particular cost pressures, as some tariﬀs on supply
chain equipment also apply to non-Chinese suppliers.
For overseas companies:
US eﬀorts to build supply chains outside China are creating
opportunities for manufacturers elsewhere. The US is

supporting rare earth production in Australia and Brazil, while
solar and battery suppliers in countries such as South Korea
and Japan could beneﬁt from stronger demand. This could
also encourage more manufacturing investment in the US.
Meanwhile, European clean energy developers could face
greater competition for non-Chinese supplies. While the EU
has not imposed US-style tariﬀs, it is tightening restrictions
on Chinese equipment through funding, procurement,

auction, and subsidy rules. This could intensify global
competition for non-Chinese suppliers and push costs higher.

PROPRIETARY RESEARCH 
EARNINGS AGGREGATES 
STOXX 600 Earnings Outlook provides analysis and commentary on aggregate earnings estimate revisions, growth rates and valuations.

Analyst: Tajinder Dhillon, CFA                                         November 5, 2026 
tajinder.dhillon@lseg.com

STOXX 600 EARNINGS OUTLOOK  
EUROPEAN AGGREGATE ESTIMATES AND REVISIONS

 Third quarter earnings are expected to increase 25.0% from Q3 2026. Excluding the Energy sector, earnings 
are expected to increase 9.5%.

 Third quarter revenue is expected to increase 11.9% from Q3 2026. Excluding the Energy sector, revenues are 
expected to increase 6.9%.

 212 companies in the STOXX 600 have reported earnings to date for Q3 2026. Of these, 58.0% reported results 
exceeding analyst estimates.

 257 companies in the STOXX 600 have reported revenue to date for Q3 2026. Of these, 64.2% reported revenue 
exceeding analyst estimates.

 During the week of November 9, 40 companies are expected to report quarterly earnings.

Through November 5, 212 companies in the STOXX 600 Index reported earnings for Q3 2026. Of these, 58% reported 
earnings above analyst estimates. In a typical quarter (sin ce 2012), 54% of companies beat estimates and 39% miss 
estimates.

In aggregate, companies are reporting earnings that are 4.2% above estimates, which is below the 5.9% long-term 
(since 2012) average surprise factor.

The estimated earnings growth rate for the STOXX 600 fo r Q3 2026 is 25%. The STOXX 600 expects to see share-
weighted earnings of €164.4B in Q3 2026, compared to shar e-weighted earnings of €131.6B (based on the year-ago 
earnings of the current constituents) in Q3 2025.

Eight of the ten sectors in the index expect to see an improvement in earnings relative to Q3 2025. The Energy sector 
has the highest earnings growth rate for the quarter, wh ile the Real Estate has the weakest anticipated growth 
compared to Q3 2025.

The Energy sector has the highest earnings growth rate (153 .4%) of any sector. It is expected to earn €35.7B in Q3 
2026, compared to earnings of €14.1B in Q3 2025.

The Real Estate sector has the lowest earnings growth rate (-69.6%) of any sector. It is expected to earn €0.9B in Q3 
2026, compared to earnings of €3.1B in Q3 2025.

sales of €196.8B in Q3 2026, compared to sales of €199.1B in Q3 2025.

HOUSEHOLD SPENDING
INDICATOR BULLETIN
Spending resilient despite headwinds
4 November 2026
Key points
• Household spending rose 0.6%mth in
September, returning to growth after
August’s brief decline. Q3 spending increased
2.1%qtr, while volumes rose a more subdued

0.7%qtr, pointing to a sharp acceleration
in price growth through the quarter.
• Services spending rebounded and goods
spending remained supportive. Non-
discretionary spending outperformed
discretionary categories, with higher fuel
spending providing significant support.
• In real terms, spending growth over the
quarter was led by transport but with
most other categories also recording
gains. All states except Tasmania
recorded positive volume growth

in Q3, led by WA and Victoria.
• Today’s volumes outcome was broadly in
line with expectations and consistent with
our consumption outlook ahead of the Q3
National Accounts (due 2 December).
Q3 real household
spending:
0.7%qtr, 3.2%yr

### Page 2

are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The monthly household spending indicator rose 0.6%mth in
September (nominal), exceeding both Westpac and market
expectations. We had anticipated a 0.5%mth contraction
(on the basis of a softer read on vehicle sales in the month),

while the market had pencilled in a 0.2%mth rise. Today’s
result instead marks a return to growth following August’s
brief contraction (revised down 0.1ppt to –0.1%mth), with
annual growth strengthening to 6.8%yr. For Q3 as a whole,
nominal spending rose 2.1%qtr, the strongest quarterly
result since the December quarter of 2022. In real terms,
volumes increased a more subdued 0.7%qtr, pointing to a
sharp acceleration in price growth through the quarter.

Non-discretionary spending outperforms
By broad category, services spending more than reversed
last month’s decline, rising a solid 1.1%mth (+6.8%yr). Goods
spending held around its recent pace, increasing 0.3%mth
(+6.7%yr). Discretionary spending returned to growth in
September, rising 0.3%mth (+6.9%yr), although this failed to
fully unwind August’s decline.
The relative strength in non-discretionary spending observed
last month continued into September, with the category rising

1.2%mth (+6.5%yr). While the headline result was stronger,
the breakdown aligns with findings from our Westpac–DataX
Card Tracker, and Westpac-DataX Consumer Panel  which
show discretionary goods continuing to underperform other
categories (see here and here).
At the detailed level, transport and health led the gains,
both rising 1.5%mth. The increase in transport spending was
largely attributable to higher fuel spending, up 5.4%mth,
reflecting elevated fuel prices, with volumes down 2.2%mth.

The ABS also noted higher spending on vehicle repairs
and maintenance, while domestic air travel increased as
households travelled to attend major sporting events.
Recreation & culture rose 0.8%mth, while miscellaneous
goods & services (+0.6%mth), hotels, cafes & restaurants
(+0.5%mth), and clothing & footwear (+0.1%mth) also
recorded gains.
Partially offsetting these increases, food spending declined
0.1%mth, while furnishings & household equipment fell

0.2%mth, likely reflecting the ongoing weakness in housing
turnover (see here). Alcoholic beverages & tobacco recorded
the largest decline, falling 0.7%mth.
State outcomes were positive across the board in September.
WA led the gains, rising 1.1%mth and reversing last
month’s decline. Victoria also rebounded (+1.0%mth),
while Queensland (+0.6%mth), SA (+0.6%mth), Tasmania
(+0.5%mth) and NSW (+0.3%mth) all recorded increases.
Spending returns to growth in September

Past performance is not a reliable indicator of future performance. The forecasts given above are predictive in character. Whilst every effort has been taken to ensure that the assumptions on which the forecasts 
are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
Volume growth remains firm 
Nominal spending rose a firm 2.1%qtr in Q3, while volumes 
increased a more subdued 0.7%qtr, matching the pace recorded 
in Q2. This implies a sharp acceleration in price growth to 
1.4%qtr, from 0.6%qtr in Q2, and marks the strongest quarterly 
increase in prices since the December quarter of 2022. Through 
the year, volume growth strengthened to 3.2%yr, remaining 
well above the 1.8%yr average recorded through 2025.
Transport led gains in volume terms, rising 1.6%qtr, 
likely partly reflecting base effects in air travel following 
disruptions associated with the Middle East conflict in the June 
quarter. Clothing & footwear (+1.2%qtr), health (+1.1%qtr), 
miscellaneous goods & services (+1.1%qtr), food (+0.5%qtr), 
recreation & culture (+0.5%qtr), and furnishings & household 
equipment (+0.1%qtr) also recorded increases. Hotels, cafes 
& restaurants spending declined 0.1%qtr in volume terms, 
while alcoholic beverages & tobacco was the main drag, falling 
1.2%qtr.
Most states recorded real spending growth over the quarter, 
led by WA (+1.4%qtr), Victoria (+0.9%qtr), NSW (+0.5%qtr), 
Queensland (+0.5%qtr) and SA (+0.4%qtr). Tasmania lagged its 
state peers, recording a 0.1%qtr decline.
Conclusion
The household spending indicator provides a useful guide to 
around two-thirds of total consumer expenditure. Importantly, 
it excludes several major categories included in the National 
Accounts measure of real household consumption, including 
rents and utilities.

Today’s volumes outcome was broadly in line with our 
expectations, with Westpac anticipating around a 0.6%qtr rise 
in Q3, and remains consistent with our view on consumption 
ahead of the Q3 National Accounts (due 2 December). 
However, this will likely be the high water mark for spending. 
The renewed deterioration in global energy markets and the 
cumulative effect of four rate increases over the first three 
quarters of the year are likely to weigh more heaveily on 
household spending in Q4.

Looking further ahead, households will continue to face 
a restrictive monetary policy environment, with the RBA 
delivering an additional rate increase at yesterday’s November 
meeting (see here). Combined with persistent consumer 
pessimism, ongoing cost-of-living pressures (see here) and a 
housing downturn impacting household wealth, spending looks 
set to slow further in 2027.

not subject to any prohibition on dealing ahead of the dissemination of
investment research.

World Trends

OPEC プラスは増産見送りを継続、2027 年の生産枠設定が次の焦点 
～足元の供給不足と将来的な供給過剰の双方をにらむ難しい舵取りが続く～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ OPEC プラス有志７ヵ国は11 月 1 日のオンライン会合で、12 月の原油生産目標を据え置き、
３ヵ月連続で増産を見送った。７ヵ国合計の生産目標は日量約3,101 万バレルである。 
○ 据え置きの背景には、イラン情勢の緊迫化がある。ホルムズ海峡などで原油輸送に支障が生
じており、目標を引き上げても実際の供給増にはつながりにくい。ＩＥＡによれば、９月の
湾岸産油国の原油輸出量は日量1,390 万バレルと、前月から増えたものの緊迫化前の約４分
の３にとどまる。 石油製品やＬＰＧの輸出は戦前の半分以下にとどまるほか、10 月には中東
のエネルギー関連施設や船舶への攻撃も再び強まった。 
○ 供給制約により原油価格は高水準で推移し、運賃や保険料の上昇も調達コストを押し上げて
いる。 一方、 高値の長期化は世界経済を下押しし、 需要減退を招く懸念がある。ＩＥＡは 2026
年の世界石油需要を前年比で日量260 万バレル減と見込んでいる。 
○ 今後の焦点は、11 月 29 日に予定される OPEC プラス閣僚会合での 2027 年の生産枠の協議と
なる。日量約 200 万バレルの協調減産の扱いや、新たな生産基準の設定が論点だが、イラン
情勢が評価作業を複雑にしている。ＩＥＡは 2027 年に供給が日量770 万バレル増える一方、
需要の回復は同 200 万バレルにとどまると予想しており、供給回復後には需給が緩む可能性
がある。ただし、OPEC とＩＥＡの需要見通しには隔たりがあり、先行きは不透明である。 
○ 日本など輸入国にとっても、原油高の長期化は貿易収支の悪化やインフレ圧力の増幅につな
がる。当面は、OPEC プラスの会合内容、ホルムズ海峡の航行状況、湾岸産油国の生産・輸出
動向、世界の石油需要を注視する必要がある。

【OPECプラス有志７ヵ国は12 月の生産目標を据え置き、３ヵ月連続で増産を見送る決定】 
OPEC（石油輸出国機構）にロシアなどの主要産油国を加えた OPEC プラスの有志７ヵ国（サウジ
アラビア、ロシア、イラク、クウェート、カザフスタン、アルジェリア、オマーン）は、11 月１日
にオンライン形式で会合を開催し、12 月の原油生産目標を11 月から据え置くことを決定した。こ
れにより、３ヵ月連続で増産が見送られることとなった。 ７ヵ国合計の生産目標は日量約3,101 万

### Page 2

2/4 
バレルとなり、９月以来の水準を維持する。今回の声明では、世界の原油市場の動向や先行きを検
討したうえで生産目標の据え置きを決定したと説明するとともに、 各国が協調減産の枠組みを順守
する姿勢をあらためて確認した。

【イラン情勢の緊迫化に伴う供給制約を背景に、生産目標と実際の生産量の乖離が続いている】 
OPEC プラス有志国は、2023 年４月に発表し、 翌５月から実施した日量165 万バレルの自主減産に
ついて、 段階的な縮小を進めてきた。2026 年９月には、 この自主減産の縮小を完了したものの、 そ
の後は 10 月、11 月と生産目標を据え置いており、今回も同様の判断を下した。背景には、イラン
情勢の緊迫化を受けて中東地域からの
原油輸送に支障が生じており、 各国が生
産目標を引き上げても、 実際の供給拡大
につながりにくい状況が続いているこ
とがある（図１）。 特に、 世界有数の原油
輸送の要衝であるホルムズ海峡では、 船
舶の航行を巡る安全上の懸念が残り、 原
油輸送に制約が生じている。 湾岸産油国
は代替輸送ルートの活用などによって
輸出の回復を図っているものの、 多くの
国の原油生産量は依然として生産目標
を下回っている。 こうした状況では、 生産目標を引き上げても実際の供給拡大につながる効果は限
定的であり、有志国は現行の生産目標を維持することが妥当と判断したと考えられる。 
実際、ＩＥＡ（国際エネルギー機関）が公表した最新の石油市場月報によれば、９月の湾岸産油
国の原油輸出量は日量1,390 万バレルと、 前月から同440 万バレル増加したものの、 イラン情勢が
緊迫化する以前の水準の４分の３程度にとどまる。さらに、原油輸出は回復する一方、石油製品や
ＬＰＧ（液化石油ガス）の輸出量は依然として戦前の半分以下にとどまり、供給制約の解消には至
っていない。10 月には中東地域のエネルギー関連施設や船舶を対象とする攻撃が再び強まり、 輸送
を巡る不確実性も高まっている。 このため、OPEC プラスが生産目標を引き上げても、 それが実際の
原油供給量の増加に結び付くとは限らず、 足元の供給不足を解消するうえでは、 イラン情勢の安定
化と原油輸送の正常化が不可欠といえる。

【原油市場では供給制約が価格を下支えする一方、高値の長期化による需要減退も懸念】 
こうした供給制約は、国際原油市況を
押し上げている（図２）。足元の原油価格
は、イラン情勢が緊迫化する以前の水準
を大きく上回るなど、原油市場では供給
不足への警戒が根強く残っている。さら
に、原油価格の高止まりに加えて海上輸
送に伴う運賃や保険料なども上昇してお
り、原油や石油製品の調達コストを押し
上げる要因となっている。こうした状況
の下では、 仮にOPEC プラスが増産に踏み
切ったとしても、供給制約が残る限り原
油価格の大幅な下落にはつながりにくいと考えられる。 
一方、 原油価格の高止まりが世界経済の下押し圧力となることも懸念される。 原油高に伴うエネ
ルギー価格の上昇は、家計の実質購買力を低下させるとともに、企業の生産・輸送コストを押し上
げることで、個人消費や設備投資などの重しとなる。IEA は、最新の石油市場月報において、2026
年の世界の石油需要が前年比で日量260 万バレル減少するとの見通しを示しており、 供給制約に伴
う石油製品の供給不足や価格高騰などが需要を下押しすると見込んでいる。 このように需要の減退
が進めば、 将来的には原油需給が緩和する可能性もある。 そのため、OPEC プラスにとっては、足元
の供給制約への対応だけでなく、 原油高が世界経済や石油需要に及ぼす影響にも配慮した政策運営
が求められる。

【今後の焦点は2027 年の生産枠設定、供給回復に伴う需給緩和の可能性も課題】 
先行きについては、OPEC プラスが11 月29 日に予定する次回会合での協議が注目される。 今回の
有志７ヵ国による会合とは異なり、次回はOPEC プラス全体の閣僚会合が予定されており、2027 年
の生産枠を巡る協議が主要な焦点になるとみられる。OPEC プラスは、2023 年４月の自主減産につ
いては９月に縮小を完了したものの、 別途実施している日量約200 万バレルの協調減産措置は2026
年末まで維持している。2027 年以降の政策運営を巡っては、 この減産措置の取り扱いが重要な論点
となる。さらに、加盟各国の持続可能な最大生産能力に関する評価を踏まえ、新たな生産基準を設
定する方針であるものの、 イラン情勢の悪化に伴う生産設備や生産能力の拡張計画への影響などが
評価作業を複雑化させている。 
その意味では、今後の OPEC プラスの政策運営は一段と難しさを増すと予想される。イラン情勢
の緊迫化が続き、 原油輸送の制約が長期化すれば、 世界的な供給不足が続くことで原油価格には上
昇圧力が掛かりやすい。一方で、停戦協議の進展などをきっかけに原油輸送が正常化すれば、これ
まで供給が制約されてきた原油が市場に流入することにより、 需給環境が一転して緩和する可能性
がある。ＩＥＡは2027 年の世界の石油供給量が前年比で日量770 万バレル増加すると見込む一方、

経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。
4/4
需要の回復幅は同200 万バレルにとどまると予想しており、 供給回復の進展に伴う需給緩和の可能
性を示唆している。もっとも、OPEC（日量１億 830 万バレル）とＩＥＡ（同１億 440 万バレル）の
需要見通しの間には大きな隔たりがあり、 需給の先行きには不確実性が残る。 したがって、OPEC プ
ラスにとっては、供給不足が続く現状と、将来的な供給過剰の双方をにらみながら、加盟国間の利
害を調整していく必要がある。
日本をはじめとする原油輸入国にとっても、 原油需給を巡る不確実性は景気や物価の先行きを左
右する重要な要因となる。 原油価格の高止まりは、 輸入額の増加を通じて貿易収支に下押し圧力を
もたらすほか、 エネルギー価格や物流コストの上昇を通じてインフレ圧力を増幅させる懸念がある。

一方、 原油輸送の正常化によって供給量が回復すれば、 原油価格の下落を通じてインフレ圧力が和
らぐことも期待される。ただし、当面は中東情勢を巡る不透明感が根強く、原油価格は地政学的な
動向に左右されやすい状況が続くと考えられる。今後は、次回の OPEC プラス閣僚会合での協議内
容に加え、ホルムズ海峡の航行状況、湾岸産油国の実際の生産・輸出動向、世界の石油需要の変化
などを注意深く見極める必要がある。

## Document 419: Threats to European diesel imports_ risk of shortages ruled out for the time being, but increased risk of price rises (2026-10-01)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Threats to European diesel imports_ risk of shortages ruled out for the time being, but increased risk of price rises (2026-10-01).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 1st October 2026

EcoFlash

Threats to European diesel imports: risk of shortages ruled out for the time being, but increased 
risk of price rises

In Europe, the possibility of a US ban on diesel exports has heightened concerns about price trends for this widely used 
fuel1, which has already risen significantly since the outbreak of the conflict in Iran. It has become a major political issue 
on both sides of the Atlantic in the run-up to decisive elections.

A particularly tight diesel market due to a shortage of buffer stocks 
Following the outbreak of the conflict, diesel prices did indeed see some of the sharpest rises on the oil market due to particularly 
tight market conditions. Since the end of February, whilst the price of a barrel of crude has risen by 46%, diesel prices have soared 
by 100% in Europe and by 76% in the United States.

The factors alleviating pressure on the refined products market, and more specifically the diesel market, were more limited than in 
the case of crude oil. Shipments passing through the Strait of Hormuz (around 17% of maritime diesel trade prior to the conf lict) 
have fallen by around 75%. Added to this is the halt in Russian exports due to war damage, which is depriving the diesel market of 
around 12% of its maritime exports. Furthermore, unlike with crude oil, China has not acted as a stabilising  force in the diesel 
market. On the contrary, the energy crisis has led to a halt in Chinese exports of refined products (including diesel) to the  rest of 
Asia from April onwards. The resumption of Chinese exports over the last two months remains fragile, given, in particular, that 
diesel stocks are at their lowest level in 15 months.

The United States has eased these tensions in the diesel market by increasing its exports of refined products (up 25% on average 
in the first half of 2026 compared with the average over the last five years). This rise in exports has required high refinery utilisation 
rate (close to maximum capacity) and, to a lesser extent, a reduction in stocks (-11% compared with the average over the last five 
years as at mid -September for middle distillates 2). All these factors have contributed to tightening the US domestic market and 
pushed retail diesel prices to record levels. Road diesel prices (US average) have risen by 68% since the end of February and by 
74% year-on-year.

A structurally high level of vulnerability in Europe… 
Against this market backdrop, characterised by mounting tensions and very limited room for manoeuvre , Europe would be 
particularly hard hit by a reduction in – or even a complete halt to – US exports. According to Eurostat, imports accounted for 46% 
of European demand for diesel in the first half of 2026, with 40% of these imports coming from the United States. Europe, which

1 Diesel accounts for 17% of the primary energy mix in the EU and 7.3% in the United States. 
2 A category of refined petroleum products comprising mainly diesel and aviation fuel.

### Page 2

has lost a great deal of production capacity in recent years, is operating its refineries at near -maximum capacity. Its room for 
manoeuvre is therefore limited.

… But the risk of a shortage has been ruled out in the short term 
The risk of a shortage, however, appears limited in the short term. The situation regarding European stocks is not comfortable, but 
it is not alarming. Commercial diesel stocks in the ARA region 3 are currently at historically low levels (around 12 million barrels), 
23% below their level at the end of September 2025. As for emergency stocks (EU Emergency Stocks), these are, according to the 
European Commission, at a sufficient level. Eurostat estim ates this figure at 270 million barrels in June 2026 (stable from a year 
earlier), representing approximately 60 days’ worth of EU consumption. Europe imports around 0.9 mb/d of diesel from the United 
States, and emergency stocks would therefore cover around 60 days’ consumption at 4.5 mb/d.

The rise in prices could be significant 
Whatever the outcome of the conflict, diesel prices are likely to continue to rise in the short term. According to our base -case 
scenario, crude oil prices are expected to remain very high in Q4 2026 due to ongoing tensions in the Persian Gulf, whilst a return 
to normal levels of diesel exports from Russia and the Middle East appears unlikely by that time.

Among the factors likely to cause future tensions, the start of the maintenance season in Europe could affect production leve ls, 
whilst demand for diesel in the United States is typically high in the autumn (agricultural harvests and pre -winter storage). I n a 
scenario where tensions remain low, the diesel refining margin (crack spread) could rise by 5% in Q4 compared with its level at the 
end of September 2026, before gradually falling back during 2027. An intermediate scenario (a 10% rise in the crack spread in Q4 
2026) would result in moderate constraints on US diesel exports and a drawdown of European stocks. Finally, an adverse scenario 
(a 35% rise in the crack spread in Q4 2026) would imply severe restrictions on US diesel exports and a delayed European response.

What impact will this have on inflation in the euro area? 
According to our estimates, the rise in diesel prices would exacerbate the expected peak in inflation in the euro area without altering 
its timing. In our baseline scenario for September 2026 (see our recently published EcoPerspective), headline inflation would peak 
at 4.1% y/y in January 2027 before gradually falling back towards 2% by the end of the year. The two least adverse scenarios 
would add only a limited amount to the peak: +0.1 pp in the first scenario (+5% for the crack spread in Q4 compared with the end 
of Q3) and +0.2 pp in the intermediate scenario (+10%), with inflation reaching 4.2% and 4.3% respectively in January 2027. 
However, there is an upside risk in the second scenario linked to the uncertain extent of the spillov er effects. In both cases, the 
main impact would be felt through fuel prices (which quickly reflect rising market prices) and would fade over the course of 2027. 
In the case of the adverse scenario (+35%), the additional inflation would be close to 0.6 pp by early 2027, pushing inflation above 
4.6%. A shock of this magnitude would, in fact, reduce businesses’ ability to absorb rising costs, which would lead to price increases 
across a broader range of goods (food, industrial goods). Subsequently, the effect would only fade gradually.

Pascal Devaux 
Economist, Middle East, Western Balkans – Energy 
pascal.devaux@bnpparibas.com

Guillaume Derrien 
Economist, Europe, Eurozone, International Trade 
guillaume.a.derrien@bnpparibas.com

3 Amsterdam-Rotterdam-Antwerp, Europe’s main hub for the storage of raw materials.
